## Document 006: 10月非农：就业市场短期修复，但结构性分化与长期失业压力仍存

10 月非农：就业市场短期修复，但结构性分化与
长期失业压力仍存

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2
近期有所走强，主要集中在会计师事务所和咨询机构。根据褐皮书的调
查，高利率环境叠加关税成本上升，推动破产相关法律服务需求增加。不
过，机构对后续业务增长仍持相对谨慎的态度，预计整体业务仅温和增
长。经济和政治不确定性，以及燃油、商品成本上升，或促使部分客户收
缩支出。此外，本月较高的就业增量可能受到选举相关活动的短期推动。
波士顿学院经济学家布莱恩·贝休恩认为，上月专业和商业服务就业激增
可能存在一定的选举因素：各竞选团队增加了对咨询、广告及临时用工的
投入，随着相关活动结束，部分岗位需求可能随之消退，预计 11 月相关
机构或裁员约 2 万人。因此，若这一判断成立，10 月专业和商业服务的
高增长可能在后续月份出现部分回吐，其持续性仍有待观察。
二是医疗和社会救助行业的就业有所回升，政策变化可能带来中期上行空
间。医疗和社会救助新增 5.5 万人，较此前有所改善，呈现一定的触底回
升迹象。其中，医疗保健服务增加 4.2 万人，高于过去 12 个月 3.1 万人

的月均水平；社会援助增加 1.4 万人。相较于 10 月的就业增量，后续政
策变化可能是影响该行业中期就业走势的另一项重要变量。中期选举后，
民主党取得参众两院控制权，医疗可负担性议题可能获得更高的立法优先
级。选前调查显示，医疗负担能力是选民关注的重要议题之一，相关选民
诉求可能推动民主党加强对《平价医疗法案》（ACA）和医疗补助计划
（Medicaid）相关政策的监督，并尝试通过立法缓解保费和药价负担。若
相关政策调整得以推进，医疗服务需求及相关就业可能获得额外支撑，医
疗行业就业的中期增长中枢或有所上移。
三是假日季前补员或带动下游就业，但上游批发却有所走弱。运输和仓
储、零售与批发均属于贸易、运输和公用事业大类。10 月这几个行业的
就业变化，可能在一定程度上反映了假日购物季前的季节性招聘，但上下
游表现并不一致。运输和仓储增加 2.5 万人，为 4 月以来最大单月增幅，
其中快递及邮政投递增加 1.7 万人。Challenger 数据显示，亚马逊 10 月
公布的 30.0 万个招聘计划几乎全部属于季节性岗位，主要集中在仓储和

电商物流领域。因此，本月运输仓储就业增长可能更多反映企业为假日购
物季提前补充人手，而非物流需求出现趋势性扩张。零售业增加 0.6 万
人，连续第八个月实现增长。分项来看，综合商品零售商和服装、服饰配
件、鞋类及珠宝零售商是主要支撑。其中，百货商店增加 0.7 万人，仓储
式会员店、大型综合超市及其他综合商品零售商合计增加 1.2 万人，服
装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商增加 1.3 万人。不过，零售就业增长同
样带有较强的季节性特征。Challenger 指出，部分零售商在裁减长期岗位
的同时，为假日购物季增加临时招聘，反映企业可能更倾向于通过季节性
用工应对短期需求，而非扩大长期雇佣规模。与运输仓储和零售业的补员
形成对比的是，批发业在连续六个月增长后，于 10 月减少 0.9 万人。其
中，耐用品批发商减少 0.3 万人，非耐用品批发商减少 0.8 万人。
四是其他行业表现仍存结构性分化：信息、金融业边际改善，休闲酒店业
有所走弱。信息业减少 0.1 万人，但近三个月就业降幅有所收窄，行业内

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3
此前负增长转为正增长。其中，金融及保险业增加 0.1 万人，房地产、租
赁及出租业增加 0.6 万人。本月信息业就业降幅收窄、金融业就业转正，
可能反映部分细分领域招聘需求有所改善。不过，信息业和金融业均受到
AI 技术发展的影响较多，目前单月数据尚不足以确认行业就业趋势已经反
转，后续仍需观察改善能否持续。休闲及酒店业减少 1.3 万人，主要受到
餐饮服务就业回落的影响。此前两个月，餐饮服务就业累计增加 4.3 万
人。但是餐饮服务业短期波动相对明显，因此本月就业下降可能包含前期
招聘增长后的季节性回吐，暂时还不足以据此判断居民服务消费需求明显
走弱。
商品业中主要是制造业支撑就业增长，新增岗位集中于 AI 相关耐用品。
商品部门新增就业约 1.5 万人，增量几乎全部来自制造业，增加 1.9 万
人，连续第五个月实现增长，而非耐用品制造业减少 0.6 万人。从需求端
看，制造业景气度也有所改善。10 月 ISM 制造业 PMI 升至 55.5，高于 9

月的 54.5 及市场预期的 54.6，创近四年来第二高的扩张水平。其中，生
产指数由 56.7 升至 59.0，新订单指数由 55.3 小幅升至 55.5。生产与新订
单指数同步处于扩张区间，表明制造业供需两端均有所改善，短期内或继
续为制造业就业提供支撑。从行业结构来看，计算机及电子产品、金属制
品和机械设备三个行业新增就业较多，合计增加 1.6 万人，或与人工智
能、数据中心、半导体及相关基础设施领域的需求增长有关。
政府就业里主要是州政府提供支撑，联邦与地方政府就业小幅收缩。政府
部门新增就业 0.6 万人，仅占全部新增非农就业的约 4%，对本月整体就
业增长的贡献相对有限。从内部结构来看，政府就业呈现分化：州政府增
加 0.8 万人，联邦政府和地方政府则分别减少 0.1 万人。
劳动力市场供需两端边际改善，但就业结构仍存分化，长期失业压力上
升。
美国劳动力需求出现边际改善，企业裁员意愿偏低，招聘活动有所回暖。
Challenger 数据显示，10 月美国雇主宣布裁员 43,994 人，略高于 9 月的

43,281 人，但较 2025 年 10 月的 153,074 人大幅下降 71%，为 2023 年
以来同月最低水平。从招聘需求来看，Indeed 招聘广告数量自 6 月触底
后缓慢回升，但扩张力度仍然有限。此外，企业也可能通过调整员工工时
而非直接裁员来应对需求变化。10 月私人非农平均每周工时维持 34.4 小
时，为 2024 年 3 月以来最高水平，企业可能通过提高现有员工的劳动投
入来满足生产需求；不过制造业平均每周工时为 40.5 小时，加班时间增
加 0.1 小时至 3.1 小时，显示需求与资本品订单的边际拉动有限。
潜在劳动力供给有所累积，就业意愿与求职信心出现背离。10 月劳动参
与率由 61.8%回升至 61.9%，劳动力人口增加 42.1 万人，表明劳动力供
给有所扩张。其中，25—54 岁核心劳动年龄人口劳动参与率为 83.7%。
与此同时，非劳动力人口内部仍存在一定的潜在供给。非劳动力人口中希
望就业但未积极求职人口的人数占比由 53.5%升至 54.5%，显示有意愿就

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 4
升至 0.43%，反映部分潜在求职者可能因就业机会不足或求职受挫而暂时
退出劳动力市场。
从就业结构来看，总量改善之下，质量仍存在分化。10 月就业人口增加
46.3 万人，高于劳动力人口增幅，意味着当月就业增长不仅吸收了新增劳
动力供给，也消化了部分存量失业人口，推动失业率由 4.2%降至 4.1%。
劳动参与率同步回升，说明失业率下降并非单纯由劳动者退出劳动力市场
所推动，就业市场总量表现有所改善。全职就业人数自 7 月近期低点以来
波动上行，显示全职岗位有所修复，但恢复过程仍不稳定。部分劳动者需
要通过同时从事多份工作来增加收入，多重职业者占总就业人口的比例升
至 5.4%，仍处于 2024 年以来 5.1%—5.5%的区间上沿。与此同时，因经
济原因从事兼职的人数环比增加 25.7 万人，反映部分劳动者仍面临全职
岗位不足或工作时长受限的问题。
从失业结构与失业时长来看，主动离职意愿偏弱，长期失业压力上升。主
动离职者占失业人口的比例由 10.5%升至 10.9%，但仍低于过去 12 个月

11.8%的均值，说明劳动者主动辞职、寻找更好工作机会的意愿尚未明显
恢复。重新进入劳动力市场者人数小幅升至 3.28 万人，高于过去 12 个月
均值，反映部分此前退出劳动力市场的人群正在重新求职。与此同时，长
期失业压力仍在累积，部分失业者重新就业的难度可能有所增加，劳动力
市场内部的就业再配置压力尚未明显缓解。10 月失业 27 周以上的人数增
加 1.9 万人至 196.3 万人，占失业人口的比例升至 27.6%，为 2021 年底
以来最高水平；失业中位时长升至 11.9 周，平均失业时长达到 27 周。
工资增长呈现环比反弹、同比放缓的分化特征。10 月私人非农平均时薪
环比上涨 0.37%，为 2025 年 11 月以来最大单月涨幅，后续仍需关注工
资环比增速能否持续维持高位；同比上涨 3.12%，仍处于缓慢回落趋势。
分行业来看，建筑业时薪环比上涨 0.91%，涨幅相对突出；制造业上涨
0.49%；专业和商业服务、教育和健康服务均上涨 0.33%。
就业短期修复提振市场，但结构性分化与持续性仍待观察。

10 月就业数据公布后，美股内部结构分化，利率重新定价与 AI 产业逻辑
交织。纳斯达克综合指数上涨 1.09%，道琼斯工业平均指数下跌 0.46%，
标普 500 指数上涨 0.31%，科技成长股明显跑赢，三大指数表现分化。债
市方面，2 年期和 10 年期美债收益率分别上行 4.9bp 和 2.4bp，收益率曲
线熊平，或反映市场对美联储降息路径重新定价，短端利率上行压力更
大。与此同时，美元指数上涨 0.68%，COMEX 黄金下跌 0.83%。整体来
看，10 月美国劳动力市场供需两端均有所改善，新增就业明显回升，失
业率下降，劳动参与率同步上升，显示就业市场短期修复力度有所增强。
但就业增长仍集中于少数行业，长期失业压力上升、非自愿兼职增加，表
明就业市场尚未实现全面、均衡的改善。往后看，就业市场回暖的持续性
仍需进一步观察，一方面，随着假日季临时招聘及选举相关用工需求逐步
消退，需关注就业增量能否保持韧性，以及招聘需求能否向更多行业扩
散；另一方面，需关注工资环比增速反弹能否延续，以及长期失业、非自

1. 就业市场短期修复，但结构性分化与长期失业压力仍存 
1.1 10 月美国就业市场明显走强，劳动力供需同步改善 
10 月美国非农就业新增 16.5 万人，大幅高于市场预期的 6.5 万人，也高于过去 12
个月 6.1 万人的月均增幅。与此同时，8 月和 9 月新增就业人数分别由 13.3 万人和 2.9
万人上修至 18.6 万人和 6.0 万人，合计上修 8.4 万人，推动近三个月新增就业均值升至
13.7 万人，为 2025 年 2 月以来最高，显示就业市场修复力度有所增强。家庭调查数据
同样表现强劲。10 月就业人口增加 46.3 万人，高于上月的 40.6 万人；U3 失业率由 4.2%
降至 4.1%，劳动参与率由 61.8%升至 61.9%。延续 7 月以来的回升趋势。劳动力参与
率回升的同时失业率有所下降，或反映劳动力市场需求有所改善。就业数据公布后，市
场对美联储政策路径的预期偏鹰：12 月加息一次的概率升至 78.8%；对于 2027 年 6 月
的政策路径，市场定价在此前加息基础上再加息一次的概率为 38.9%，维持利率不变的
概率为 33.9%，两者差距较小，表明市场虽倾向于进一步加息，但对后续政策路径仍存
在较大分歧。 
1.2 行业扩散有所改善，但新增就业仍集中于少数领域，结构性分化明显 
10 月非农新增就业 16.5 万人，其中私人部门增加 15.9 万人，政府部门增加 0.6 万
人，新增就业几乎全部来自私人部门。而私人部门新增就业主要由服务部门贡献，增加
14.4 万人。从就业扩散情况看，行业扩散指数由 9 月修正后的 54.2 升至 57.6，三个月
均值也升至今年以来最高水平，表明新增就业涉及的行业范围有所扩大。不过，从具体
行业贡献来看，就业增长仍存在明显的结构性分化：专业和商业服务、医疗和社会救助、
运输和仓储三大行业合计新增约 15 万人，占全部新增非农就业的九成以上，表明本轮
就业增长仍主要由少数行业支撑，尚未形成广泛、均衡的招聘回暖。 
服务业方面主要呈现四大特征： 
一是专业和商业服务贡献最大，但增量可能包含一次性因素。专业和商业服务新增
7.0 万人，占全部新增非农就业的 42.4%，为 2022 年 7 月以来最大单月增幅，明显高于
过去 12 个月 1.5 万人的月均水平。分项来看，专业、科学和技术服务增加 3.3 万人，其
中管理、科学和技术咨询服务贡献 1.7 万人；行政和支持服务增加 2.8 万人，其中临时
帮助服务增加 1.9 万人；企业管理增加 0.9 万人。从需求端反馈来看，专业和商业服务
需求近期有所走强，主要集中在会计师事务所和咨询机构。根据褐皮书的调查，高利率
环境叠加关税成本上升，推动破产相关法律服务需求增加。不过，机构对后续业务增长
仍持相对谨慎的态度，预计整体业务仅温和增长。经济和政治不确定性，以及燃油、商
品成本上升，或促使部分客户收缩支出1。此外，本月较高的就业增量可能受到选举相关
活动的短期推动。波士顿学院经济学家布莱恩·贝休恩认为，上月专业和商业服务就业激
增可能存在一定的选举因素：各竞选团队增加了对咨询、广告及临时用工的投入，随着
相关活动结束，部分岗位需求可能随之消退，预计 11 月相关机构或裁员约 2 万人2。因
此，若这一判断成立，10 月专业和商业服务的高增长可能在后续月份出现部分回吐，其
持续性仍有待观察。 
二是医疗和社会救助行业的就业有所回升，政策变化可能带来中期上行空间。医疗
和社会救助新增 5.5 万人，较此前有所改善，呈现一定的触底回升迹象。其中，医疗保
健服务增加 4.2 万人，高于过去 12 个月 3.1 万人的月均水平；社会援助增加 1.4 万人。
相较于 10 月的就业增量，后续政策变化可能是影响该行业中期就业走势的另一项重要
变量。中期选举后，民主党取得参众两院控制权，医疗可负担性议题可能获得更高的立
法优先级。选前调查显示，医疗负担能力是选民关注的重要议题之一，相关选民诉求可
能推动民主党加强对《平价医疗法案》（ACA）和医疗补助计划（Medicaid）相关政策的

### Page 9

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 9 
监督，并尝试通过立法缓解保费和药价负担3。若相关政策调整得以推进，医疗服务需求
及相关就业可能获得额外支撑，医疗行业就业的中期增长中枢或有所上移。 
三是假日季前补员或带动下游就业，但上游批发却有所走弱。运输和仓储、零售与
批发均属于贸易、运输和公用事业大类。10 月这几个行业的就业变化，可能在一定程度
上反映了假日购物季前的季节性招聘，但上下游表现并不一致。运输和仓储增加 2.5 万
人，为 4 月以来最大单月增幅，其中快递及邮政投递增加 1.7 万人。Challenger 数据显
示，亚马逊 10 月公布的 30.0 万个招聘计划几乎全部属于季节性岗位，主要集中在仓储
和电商物流领域4。因此，本月运输仓储就业增长可能更多反映企业为假日购物季提前补
充人手，而非物流需求出现趋势性扩张。零售业增加 0.6 万人，连续第八个月实现增长。
分项来看，综合商品零售商和服装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商是主要支撑。其中，
百货商店增加 0.7 万人，仓储式会员店、大型综合超市及其他综合商品零售商合计增加
1.2 万人，服装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商增加 1.3 万人。不过，零售就业增长同样
带有较强的季节性特征。Challenger 指出，部分零售商在裁减长期岗位的同时，为假日
购物季增加临时招聘，反映企业可能更倾向于通过季节性用工应对短期需求，而非扩大
长期雇佣规模。与运输仓储和零售业的补员形成对比的是，批发业在连续六个月增长后，
于 10 月减少 0.9 万人。其中，耐用品批发商减少 0.3 万人，非耐用品批发商减少 0.8 万
人。 
四是其他行业表现仍存结构性分化：信息、金融业边际改善，休闲酒店业有所走弱。
信息业减少 0.1 万人，但近三个月就业降幅有所收窄，行业内部也出现一定分化。其中，
计算基础设施、数据处理、网络托管及相关服务就业持平，电影及录音产业增加 0.3 万
人。金融业则增加 0.7 万人，由此前负增长转为正增长。其中，金融及保险业增加 0.1
万人，房地产、租赁及出租业增加 0.6 万人。本月信息业就业降幅收窄、金融业就业转
正，可能反映部分细分领域招聘需求有所改善。不过，信息业和金融业均受到 AI 技术发
展的影响较多，目前单月数据尚不足以确认行业就业趋势已经反转，后续仍需观察改善
能否持续。休闲及酒店业减少 1.3 万人，主要受到餐饮服务就业回落的影响。此前两个
月，餐饮服务就业累计增加 4.3 万人。但是餐饮服务业短期波动相对明显，因此本月就
业下降可能包含前期招聘增长后的季节性回吐，暂时还不足以据此判断居民服务消费需
求明显走弱。 
商品业中主要是制造业支撑就业增长，新增岗位集中于 AI 相关耐用品。商品部门
新增就业约 1.5 万人，增量几乎全部来自制造业，增加 1.9 万人，连续第五个月实现增
长，而非耐用品制造业减少 0.6 万人。从需求端看，制造业景气度也有所改善。10 月 ISM
制造业 PMI 升至 55.5，高于 9 月的 54.5 及市场预期的 54.6，创近四年来第二高的扩张
水平。其中，生产指数由 56.7 升至 59.0，新订单指数由 55.3 小幅升至 55.5。生产与新
订单指数同步处于扩张区间，表明制造业供需两端均有所改善，短期内或继续为制造业
就业提供支撑。从行业结构来看，计算机及电子产品、金属制品和机械设备三个行业新
增就业较多，合计增加 1.6 万人，或与人工智能、数据中心、半导体及相关基础设施领
域的需求增长有关。 
政府就业里主要是州政府提供支撑，联邦与地方政府就业小幅收缩。政府部门新增
就业 0.6 万人，仅占全部新增非农就业的约 4%，对本月整体就业增长的贡献相对有限。
从内部结构来看，政府就业呈现分化：州政府增加 0.8 万人，联邦政府和地方政府则分
别减少 0.1 万人。 
1.3 劳动力市场供需两端边际改善，但就业结构仍存分化，长期失业压力
上升 
美国劳动力需求出现边际改善，企业裁员意愿偏低，招聘活动有所回暖。Challenger

### Page 10

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 10
数据显示，10 月美国雇主宣布裁员 43,994 人，略高于 9 月的 43,281 人，但较 2025 年
10 月的 153,074 人大幅下降 71%，为 2023 年以来同月最低水平。从招聘需求来看，
Indeed 招聘广告数量自 6 月触底后缓慢回升，但扩张力度仍然有限。此外，企业也可能
通过调整员工工时而非直接裁员来应对需求变化。10 月私人非农平均每周工时维持 34.4
小时，为 2024 年 3 月以来最高水平，企业可能通过提高现有员工的劳动投入来满足生
产需求；不过制造业平均每周工时为 40.5 小时，加班时间增加 0.1 小时至 3.1 小时，显
示需求与资本品订单的边际拉动有限。
潜在劳动力供给有所累积，就业意愿与求职信心出现背离。10 月劳动参与率由 61.8%
回升至 61.9%，劳动力人口增加 42.1 万人，表明劳动力供给有所扩张。其中，25—54
岁核心劳动年龄人口劳动参与率为 83.7%。与此同时，非劳动力人口内部仍存在一定的

潜在供给。非劳动力人口中希望就业但未积极求职人口的人数占比由 53.5%升至 54.5%，
显示有意愿就业但尚未进入劳动力市场的人群有所增加，后续若就业机会改善，部分非
劳动力人口或重新进入劳动力市场。与此同时，失望个人占比从 0.37%上升至 0.43%，
反映部分潜在求职者可能因就业机会不足或求职受挫而暂时退出劳动力市场。
从就业结构来看，总量改善之下，质量仍存在分化。10 月就业人口增加 46.3 万人，
高于劳动力人口增幅，意味着当月就业增长不仅吸收了新增劳动力供给，也消化了部分
存量失业人口，推动失业率由 4.2%降至 4.1%。劳动参与率同步回升，说明失业率下降
并非单纯由劳动者退出劳动力市场所推动，就业市场总量表现有所改善。全职就业人数
自 7 月近期低点以来波动上行，显示全职岗位有所修复，但恢复过程仍不稳定。部分劳
动者需要通过同时从事多份工作来增加收入，多重职业者占总就业人口的比例升至 5.4%，
仍处于 2024 年以来 5.1%—5.5%的区间上沿。与此同时，因经济原因从事兼职的人数

环比增加 25.7 万人，反映部分劳动者仍面临全职岗位不足或工作时长受限的问题。
从失业结构与失业时长来看，主动离职意愿偏弱，长期失业压力上升。主动离职者
占失业人口的比例由 10.5%升至 10.9%，但仍低于过去 12 个月 11.8%的均值，说明劳
动者主动辞职、寻找更好工作机会的意愿尚未明显恢复。重新进入劳动力市场者人数小
幅升至 3.28 万人，高于过去 12 个月均值，反映部分此前退出劳动力市场的人群正在重
新求职。与此同时，长期失业压力仍在累积，部分失业者重新就业的难度可能有所增加，
劳动力市场内部的就业再配置压力尚未明显缓解。10 月失业 27 周以上的人数增加 1.9
万人至 196.3 万人，占失业人口的比例升至 27.6%，为 2021 年底以来最高水平；失业
中位时长升至 11.9 周，平均失业时长达到 27 周。
工资增长呈现环比反弹、同比放缓的分化特征。10 月私人非农平均时薪环比上涨
0.37%，为 2025 年 11 月以来最大单月涨幅，后续仍需关注工资环比增速能否持续维持

高位；同比上涨 3.12%，仍处于缓慢回落趋势。分行业来看，建筑业时薪环比上涨 0.91%，
涨幅相对突出；制造业上涨 0.49%；专业和商业服务、教育和健康服务均上涨 0.33%。
1.4 就业短期修复提振市场，但结构性分化与持续性仍待观察
10 月就业数据公布后，美股内部结构分化，利率重新定价与 AI 产业逻辑交织。纳
斯达克综合指数上涨 1.09%，道琼斯工业平均指数下跌 0.46%，标普 500 指数上涨 0.31%，
科技成长股明显跑赢，三大指数表现分化。债市方面，2 年期和 10 年期美债收益率分别
上行 4.9bp 和 2.4bp，收益率曲线熊平，或反映市场对美联储降息路径重新定价，短端
利率上行压力更大。与此同时，美元指数上涨 0.68%，COMEX 黄金下跌 0.83%。整体
来看，10 月美国劳动力市场供需两端均有所改善，新增就业明显回升，失业率下降，劳
动参与率同步上升，显示就业市场短期修复力度有所增强。但就业增长仍集中于少数行
业，长期失业压力上升、非自愿兼职增加，表明就业市场尚未实现全面、均衡的改善。

投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 084: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の 73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業 175 社の 2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」
（注）26年冬の予測値は第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所

26Q3 受惠終端需求強勁，營收達 81.10 億元，QoQ 8.01%，YoY 
35.27%；受惠稼動率提升與價格策略帶動毛利走高，單季 EPS 達
3.48 元。10 月營收延續強勁動能，達 28.50 億元，MoM 4.79%，
YoY 35.66%，累計前 10 月營收為 248.48 億元，YoY 25.11%。
目前整體市場供不應求，訂單能見度已直達 2027 年中。 
展望後市，隨公司下半年增聘逾 300 名員工並持續加班生產，26
年底產出量將較年初成長 25%至 30%，預期 26Q4 將打破歐美長
假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 26Q3。研究部預估 26Q4 營收
為 85.10 億元，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；毛利率估為 35.11%，
營業利益 12.53 億元，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 297.33%；單季 EPS
為 2.95 元。綜合考量下游客戶需求持續暢旺、公司優先承接高毛
利訂單使獲利結構性優化，並已長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機，研
究部預估 26 與 27 年營收為 305.08 億元與 344.40 億元，YoY 
25.74%與 12.89%；毛利率為 34.54%與 35.11%；營業利益為 43.63
億元與 58.60 億元，YoY 165.52%與 34.30%；稅後獲利為 36.93
億元與 48.07 億元，YoY 142.05%與 30.15%；預估 26 與 27 年
EPS 分別為 10.44 元與 13.59 元。 
評價方面，上銀過去五年本益比主要集中於 20 倍至 40 倍區間。
考量公司整體營收與獲利將呈現雙位數之成長上升趨勢，且自動化
與傳動產業結構持續復甦上行，研究部以 2027 年預估 EPS 13.59
元作為評價基礎，並給予歷史區間中緣偏上之 33 倍 PER，目標價
設定為 450 元，給予買進之投資建議。

上銀科技(2049)成立於 1989 年，主要從事線性滑軌(GW)、滾
螺桿(BS)、工業機器人、機器手臂、迴轉工作台、減速機、精
密軸承及醫療設備之研究開發、設計、製造、銷售、維修及售
前售後服務，同時為全球第三大滾珠螺桿廠和線性滑軌廠，僅
次於日本 THK、NSK。公司以自有品牌「HIWIN」行銷全球，
海外子公司橫跨德國、美國、日本、瑞士、捷克、法國，

⚫ 26Q4 有望淡季不淡，估營收季增 4.94%達 85.10 億 
上銀 10 月營收 28.50，YoY 35.66%，MoM4.79%，累計
營收為 248.48 億， YoY25.11%。目前下游需求仍強勁，政
體市場處供不應求之態勢，隨 26H2 增聘逾 300 名員工並採
加班生產，26 年底產出量較年初成長 25%～30%，預期 
26Q4 有望突破歐美長假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 
26Q3。 
研究部預估，26Q4 營收 85.10 億，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；
毛利率 35.11%，季減 1.23 個百分點，年增 8.07 個百分點；
營業利益 12.53 億，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 297.33%；稅後獲
利 10.45 億，QoQ -14.42%，YoY 126.22%；EPS 為 2.95 元。

⚫ 訂單滿載，能見度達 2027 年中 
估計 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 10.44/13.59 元。 
目前下游需求仍呈現供不應求，研磨級滾珠螺桿交期已拉長至 
6.5 個月以上，線性滑軌依產品規格亦維持 4～6 個月，整體
訂單能見度直達 2027 年中，且 26Q3 合約負債達 1.34 億
元（YoY +126%）。 
各區域表現方面，亞洲市場受惠中國大陸工具機產業升級，帶
動傳動元件需求持續成長，同時台灣與亞洲各國受惠半導體製
造需求熱絡，推升機器人、馬達與旋轉轉台等零組件出貨；美
國受惠製造業回流、半導體擴產與 AI 資料中心建置潮，帶動
強勁設備需求；歐洲雖受車市疲軟影響而整體持平，但軍工與
潛在戰後重建需求已逐步浮現。 
獲利與產能方面，管理層表示現階段不傾向全面漲價，而採取
融資率 4.25 -0.09

⚫ 長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機 
機器人業務佔 26Q3 營收達 12%，其中 EFEM 隨半導體需
求熱絡穩健成長，並已超前部署對應 FOPLP 方片規格之移載
系統。公司充分發揮垂直整合優勢，自製減速機、螺桿、軸承，
並整合關係企業大銀微系統之伺服馬達與驅動器。目前已獲美
系人形機器人廠商小批量線性滑軌訂單，美系 AI 物流機器人
放量時程則取決於客戶端實測進度。CPO 方面，大銀微已開
發出奈米級定位平台，並整合上銀微小型螺桿與線軌，共同搶
攻次世代半導體設備商機。

2027 年中。

間。考量公司整體營收與獲利將呈現雙位數之成長上升趨勢，

Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
In-line 3Q26 results
Co-Tech reported 3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y).
• GM was 26.7% (-70bps q/q, +620bps y/y), 20/150bps below MSe/Street,
which we attributed to higher copper pricing and electricity rates (link).
• OP came in at NT$757mn (+11% q/q, +125% y/y), 1% above MSe but 5% below
the Street, for OpM of 23.9% (-50bps q/q, +690bps y/y), 30bps above MSe
but 140bps below the Street.

• As a result, NP was NT$608mn (+12% q/q, +140% y/y), in line with MSe but
4% below the Street, for EPS of NT$2.41.
• Co-Tech’s board also approved up to NT$7bn of CBs to fund the company's
Malaysian expansion. Issue price will be set by auction. Conversion price is
TBD pending regulatory approval.
• Reiterate OW; PT NT$745.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13
Sep 2026)

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 143: 261110_ubs_allring

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_ubs_allring.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
All Ring Tech
Bright 2027 outlook with multiple drivers
Intact 2027 outlook
All Ring's 2027 outlook is robust, supported by three key drivers. 1) We expect TSMC
and ASE to continue accelerating CoWoS capacity expansion, with industry capacity
reaching ~270kwpm by end-2027E, up from ~160kwpm by end-2026. We estimate
TSMC and ASE’s capacity could reach ~180kwpm and ~70kwpm, respectively, by
2027E. 2) Second, with Intel's EMIB-T progressing on track toward volume production

by early 2028, we expect related equipment demand to begin ramping in 2027,
providing a new growth driver for All Ring. 3) CPO’s fiber array unit (FAU) coupling tools
start contributing to All Ring's 2027 revenue, with demand from Nvidia and AMD.
More concrete CPO development in 2027-28
Our industry feedback suggests Nvidia is taking the lead to aggressively push CPO mass
production in 2027 for scale up switch solution. AMD is also engaging with key

equipment suppliers, in preparation for competing CPO solution. Competition among
the equipment vendors is fierce, but we believe All Ring is well positioned in FAU
coupling as a tier 1 supplier, given the superior execution in driving higher tool
throughput versus peers. Considering the meaningful tool demand in 2027-28, we
forecast All Ring to recognize ~100 tools for revenue in 2027E, contributing ~12% of All
Ring's sales next year. Potential upside to our current estimates could come from larger

TAM for faster capacity expansion, All Ring securing higher share / ASP through better
tool performance.
Lower Q3 gross margin due to mix
Q3 EPS came in below LSEG consensus by -10% as gross margin of 48.5% missed
consensus of 54.2%. Lower gross margin could be due to a higher mix of automation
products, which carries a lower margin vs corporate average. For Q4, considering lighter
seasonality and current visibility, we model sales to decline by -28% from Q3 with gross

margin of 53% and EPS of NT$7.57.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and target of $1700
Post Q3 results, we fine-tune 2026 EPS by -4% due to lower gross margin in the quarter,
though our estimates for 2027/28 are largely kept unchanged. Our 2027E/2028E EPS
are 3%/30% ahead of Consensus pre-earnings. We maintain our Buy rating and price
target of $1700 based on 27x average 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based supplier of automation equipment for the
semiconductor back-end packaging and passive component manufacturing industries. The
company's products include underfill dispensers, heatsink placement and automated optical
inspection (AOI) tools for advanced packaging equipment, particularly for heterogeneous

integration. Revenue is generated primarily from sales of semiconductor and electronic
manufacturing equipment and related automation solutions, with more than 90% of
revenue derived from Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value All Ring based on PE methodology.
Risks for All Ring and the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in

the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech

companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in All Ring
gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in All
Ring losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could

lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to All
Ring, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact All Ring’s
margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 276: Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets

Source PDF: all report pdf/Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Given the level of uncertainty in energy markets this year and
with little sign of a change going into next year, any call on
energy markets for 2027 is a low-conviction call. But here are
our 3 calls for energy markets for 2027
Warren Patterson
Oil ﬂows are recovering, but uncertainty remains
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Brent to average
$77/bbl next year
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.

Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Oil markets have been on a roller coaster this year given
events in the Middle East. However, we are going into year-
end with oil ﬂows from the region near pre-war levels despite
the lack of a deal between the US and Iran. While oil ﬂows are
normalising, the way oil is coming out of the region is
anything but normal, which is reﬂected in the surge in tanker
shipping rates, given the very ineﬃcient oil ﬂows.
Our base case assumes occasional ﬂare-ups between the US

and Iran, meaning a full normalisation of Persian Gulf
supplies remains some way oﬀ and the oil market is likely to
retain a degree of risk premium. However, provided there are
no signiﬁcant or prolonged disruptions, we expect the market
surplus to build over the course of 2027, leaving Brent to
average around $77/bbl. A deal between the US and Iran
would add further downside risk to this view.
Our risky call: OPEC will see more member
exits
We believe harmony within OPEC could be tested, with the

possibility of further members leaving the group. This follows the
UAE’s departure earlier this year. Post-Persian Gulf disruptions, any
breakdown or weakening of the group risks adding more supply to
the market, which would potentially leave further downside to our
base case price forecasts for Brent in 2027.
The most likely candidate to leave OPEC is Venezuela, with the US’s
growing inﬂuence over the Venezuelan oil industry following the
capture and arrest of President Maduro at the start of 2026. But we

believe that some Persian Gulf producers may also question their
membership, particularly if disagreements emerge over the
capacity numbers used by the group to set production targets. Iraq
has already made noise around production targets, and following
the exit of the UAE, some members may be looking at them
enviously, with the UAE pumping record volumes of crude since
leaving the bloc.

Read more
Download
Our bold call: TTF to trade sub-€40/MWh
before the end of next year
European gas prices have strengthened signiﬁcantly in 2026. But

what goes up must come down, and we believe prices could fall
sharply next year. There are clearly plenty of assumptions here,
most importantly a normalisation of Persian Gulf LNG ﬂows in
early 2027. If that happens, we believe TTF could trade below
€40/MWh by end-2027.
On the demand side, higher prices are already driving some
industrial demand destruction, while we are deep in the gas-to-coal
switching range. Furthermore, El Niño could bring milder weather to

parts of Europe and East Asia. While far from guaranteed, this could
reduce heating demand in winter. So, we potentially come out of
the 2026/27 winter with the EU gas balance not as tight as many
fear, putting downward pressure on prices.
We also have signiﬁcant US LNG export capacity coming onto the
market, which should help with storage ahead of the 2027/28
winter. If the situation in the Persian Gulf normalises, we also have
new capacity starting up in Qatar.

[Nhận định xu hướng]

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh khoản mở 
rộng. Thiết bị dụng cụ Y tế, Tiện ích khí và Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 
-870 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã MBB, VIC và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
ở mức 504 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -2,010 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -10,888 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 1,208 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -4,070 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần giảm điểm thứ 2 liên tiếp khi VN-Index lùi về kiểm định vùng hỗ trợ 
quanh 1.725 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm giảm hơn 30 điểm trước khi thu hẹp 
đà giảm nhờ lực cầu ở nhóm chứng khoán. Áp lực bán tập trung ở nhóm vốn hóa lớn, đặc 
biệt là bộ đôi Vingroup (VIC, VHM) cùng một số cổ phiếu ngân hàng và công nghệ, khiến 
chỉ số thiếu lực đỡ. Giao dịch của khối ngoại diễn biến trái chiều và giằng co — có phiên 
mua ròng trở lại ở một số mã, nhưng vẫn xen kẽ bán ròng ở nhóm trụ như VHM, VCB, cho 
thấy dòng vốn ngoại chưa xác lập xu hướng rõ ràng. Bối cảnh bên ngoài kém thuận lợi khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu công nghệ tại Mỹ bị bán tháo, kéo theo các chỉ số chính toàn cầu đồng 
loạt lao dốc. Mặc dù vậy, kỳ vọng vào động thái mở kênh hoán đổi ngoại tệ của NHNN để 
hỗ trợ thanh khoản, hạ lãi suất đã giúp tâm lý nhà đầu tư trong nước ổn định và không 
xuất hiện áp lực bán tháo.

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến spinning thân nhỏ với bóng trên/dưới khá dài, 
cùng thanh khoản gia tăng, thể hiện sự giằng co mạnh nhưng về tổng thể lại cho thấy diễn 
biến đã cân bằng trở lại sau tuần giảm điểm trước đó. Trên khung ngày, mặc dù trải qua 
các phiên biến động tăng/giảm mạnh đan xen nhưng việc cho phản ứng bật tăng tích cực 
tại quanh đường biên dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn cho thấy tương quan 
vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều hướng tăng. Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn 
tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ tiếp tục hướng lên thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1800 (+-15)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

Exhibits 4A and 4B) Source: Bloomberg. Data as of October 30, 2026. Past performance is no guarantee of future results. Please refer to index definitions at the end of this report. It is
not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integrating Europe’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after

its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as“not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
The upcoming local elections could serve as a political barometer ahead of the 2028 general election–

vote, the results can provide a sense of the domestic balance of power.
Elections in Taiwan’s six special municipalities – Taipei, New Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, Taoyuan, and

Spain’s snap general election could mark a major turning point for Spanish politics and the EU,
potentially ending eight years of Socialist-led government under Pedro Sánchez and bringing the
conservative People’s Party (PP) to power with support from the far-right Vox. Polling suggests PP is
ahead and a PP-Vox alignment is the only one projected to reach a majority. A rightward shift in one of
the EU’s largest economies could occur just as Europe enters a wider electoral cycle, potentially

complicating consensus on migration, climate policy, Ukraine and European integration.
The Forum comes at a pivotal moment in EU-Latin American relations. It is the EU’s first big commercial
push into Argentina since the EU-Mercosur trade deal began to be provisionally applied on 1 May. The
agreement opens new opportunities for trade, investment and market access. The EU is Argentina’s
largest source of foreign direct investment. Around 800 government, business and financial leaders will

focus on energy, critical minerals, agriculture and infrastructure. The EU’s Global Gateway investment
agenda provides a framework for mobilizing public and private capital. While a key outcome will be the
launch of a structured EU-Argentina investment dialogue, to watch will be the ability of Argentine
officials to allay concerns around policy stability and regulatory predictability.

## Document 388: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15

nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
